===== .devcontainer/devcontainer.json =====
{
  "name": "LaTeX",
  "image": "texlive/texlive:latest-full",
  "customizations": {
    "vscode": {
      "extensions": [
        "James-Yu.latex-workshop"
      ]
    }
  },
  "postCreateCommand": "tlmgr update --self && tlmgr install acronym pgf && texhash"
}

\documentclass[11pt, a4paper]{article}
\usepackage{style-cv-picture-template}

\begin{document}

% ==============================================================================
% CONTACT INFORMATION & TECHNICAL LINKS
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Name, #2: Location URL, #3: Location Name, #4: Email, #5: Phone, #6: LinkedIn URL, #7: LinkedIn Name, #8: GitHub URL, #9: GitHub Name

\cvProfilePic{image.jpg}

\cvHeader
    {Your Name}
    {https://map.location}
    {City}
    {email@example.com}
    {+1 234 567 890}
    {https://linkedin.com/in/yourprofile}
    {linkedin.com/in/yourprofile}
    {https://github.com/yourgithub}
    {github.com/yourgithub}

% --- Commented Example: Adding additional header links (e.g., Portfolio) ---
% Because \cvHeader already takes the maximum of 9 arguments, you can add 
% extra links like a Portfolio by uncommenting and modifying the example 
% block at the end of the \cvHeader definition in cv.sty.

% ==============================================================================
% TECHNICAL PROFILE / PROFESSIONAL SUMMARY (OPTIONAL)
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Summary Text

\section{Summary}
\cvSummary{Provide a brief 2-3 sentence summary highlighting your core technical skills, research interests, and what you aim to achieve in your academic or professional career.}

% ==============================================================================
% EDUCATION
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Degree, #2: University, #3: Date, #4: Location, #5: Bullet Points

\section{Education}
\cvEducation
    {Degree Name in Major}
    {University or Institution Name}
    {Month, Year – Month, Year}
    {City, Country}
    {
        \item \textbf{Relevant Coursework:} Course 1, Course 2, Course 3, Course 4
        \item \textbf{Thesis:} Title of your thesis or research focus
    }

% ==============================================================================
% CORE COMPETENCIES & TECHNICAL SKILLS
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Category, #2: Skills List

\cvSkill{Programming Languages}{Skill 1, Skill 2, Skill 3, Skill 4, Skill 5}
\cvSkill{Frameworks \& Libraries}{Skill 1, Skill 2, Skill 3, Skill 4, Skill 5}
\cvSkill{Tools \& Technologies}{Skill 1, Skill 2, Skill 3, Skill 4, Skill 5}

% ==============================================================================

    }

===== cv_old.sty =====
\NeedsTeXFormat{LaTeX2e}
\ProvidesPackage{cv}[2026/06/08 Abstracted CV Style]

% --- Core Packages ---
\RequirePackage[
    ignoreheadfoot, % Set margins without considering header and footer
    % top=0.5in, bottom=0.5in, left=0.5in, right=0.5in
    top=1in, bottom=1in, left=1in, right=1in
]{geometry}
\RequirePackage{titlesec} % For customizing section titles
\RequirePackage{tabularx} % For tables
\RequirePackage{array}
\RequirePackage[dvipsnames]{xcolor} % For text colors
\RequirePackage{enumitem} % For list customization
\RequirePackage{fontawesome5} % For icons
\RequirePackage{accsupp} % For ATS text replacement
\RequirePackage{amsmath}
\RequirePackage{calc}
\RequirePackage{changepage} % For single column entries
\RequirePackage{paracol} % For two column layouts
\RequirePackage{ifthen}
\RequirePackage{needspace} % Prevent page breaks after titles
\RequirePackage{iftex}

% --- ATS Parsable PDF Setup ---
\ifPDFTeX
    \input{glyphtounicode}
    \pdfgentounicode=1
    \RequirePackage[utf8]{inputenc}
    \RequirePackage{lmodern}
\fi

% --- Colors & Links ---
\definecolor{primaryColor}{RGB}{0, 79, 144}
\RequirePackage[
    colorlinks=true,
    urlcolor=primaryColor,
    pdftitle={CV},
    pdfauthor={Your Name}
]{hyperref}

% Custom link command with an external arrow icon
\let\hrefWithoutArrow\href
% \renewcommand{\href}[2]{\hrefWithoutArrow{#1}{\ifthenelse{\equal{#2}{}}{ }{#2 }\raisebox{.15ex}{\footnotesize \faExternalLink*}}}

% --- Global Formatting ---
\pagestyle{empty} % Remove page numbers
\setcounter{secnumdepth}{0} % No section numbers
\setlength{\parindent}{0pt} % Remove indentation
\setlength{\topskip}{0pt}
\setlength{\columnsep}{0cm} % Column separation for paracol
\AtBeginEnvironment{adjustwidth}{\partopsep0pt}

% --- Section Styling ---
\titleformat{\section}{\needspace{4\baselineskip}\bfseries\large}{}{0pt}{}[\vspace{1pt}\titlerule]
\titlespacing{\section}{0pt}{0.15cm}{0.2cm}

% --- Content Environments (Hidden from main file) ---
\newenvironment{cvheader}{
    \setlength{\topsep}{0pt}\par\kern\topsep\centering\linespread{1.5}
}{\par\kern\topsep}

\renewcommand\labelitemi{$\circ$} % Custom bullet points

\newenvironment{highlights}{
    \begin{itemize}[topsep=0.05cm, parsep=0.03cm, partopsep=0pt, itemsep=0pt, leftmargin=0.4cm+10pt]
}{\end{itemize}}

\newenvironment{onecolentry}{
    \begin{adjustwidth}{0.2cm+0.00001cm}{0.2cm+0.00001cm}
}{\end{adjustwidth}}

\newenvironment{twocolentry}[2][]{
    \onecolentry
    \def\secondColumn{#2}
    \setcolumnwidth{\fill, 6cm}
    \begin{paracol}{2}
}{
    \switchcolumn \raggedleft \secondColumn
    \end{paracol}
    \endonecolentry
}

% --- ATS-Friendly Icon Wrapper ---
\newcommand{\atsIcon}[2]{%
    \BeginAccSupp{method=pdfstringdef,unicode,ActualText=#1}%
    #2%
    \EndAccSupp{}%
}

% ==============================================================================
% SEMANTIC DATA COMMANDS (To be used in cv.tex)
% ==============================================================================

% Header Variables
\newcommand{\contactItem}[3]{%
    \mbox{\hrefWithoutArrow{#1}{\color{black}{\footnotesize #2}\hspace*{0.10cm}#3}}%
}

% Header Variables - with hidden ATS labels
% \newcommand{\contactItem}[4]{%
%     \mbox{\hrefWithoutArrow{#1}{\color{black}{\footnotesize #2}\hspace*{0.10cm}#3\phantom{\footnotesize#4}}}%
% }

\newsavebox\ANDbox
\sbox\ANDbox{}
\newcommand{\contactSeparator}{%
    \kern 0.15cm\unskip\cleaders\copy\ANDbox\hskip\wd\ANDbox\ignorespaces\kern 0.15cm%
}

\newcommand{\makeheader}[2]{
    \begin{cvheader}
        \textbf{\fontsize{24 pt}{24 pt}\selectfont #1}
        \vspace{0.3 cm}
        
        \fontsize{9.5}{11}\selectfont #2
    \end{cvheader}
    \vspace{0.2cm}
}

\newboolean{isFirstContactItem}
% #1: Name, #2: Location URL, #3: Location Name, #4: Email, #5: Phone, #6: LinkedIn URL, #7: LinkedIn Name, #8: GitHub URL, #9: GitHub Name
\newcommand{\cvHeader}[9]{
    \setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{true}%
    \makeheader{#1}{%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#3}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{#2}{\atsIcon{Location: }{\faMapMarker*}}{#3}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#4}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{mailto:#4}{\atsIcon{Email: }{\faEnvelope[regular]}}{#4}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#5}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{tel:#5}{\atsIcon{Phone: }{\faPhone*}}{#5}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#7}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{#6}{\atsIcon{LinkedIn: }{\faLinkedinIn}}{#7}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#9}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{#8}{\atsIcon{GitHub: }{\faGithub}}{#9}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
        \contactItem{https://medium.com/@ItsTHEAvro}{\atsIcon{Medium: }{\faMedium}}{medium.com/@ItsTHEAvro}%
        % % Uncomment and modify the lines below to add a portfolio or other link
        % \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
        % \contactItem{https://yourportfolio.com}{\atsIcon{Portfolio: }{\faGlobe}}{yourportfolio.com}%
        % ----------------------------------------------------------------------
    }%
}

% #1: Summary Text
\newcommand{\cvSummary}[1]{
    \begin{onecolentry}
        #1
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Degree, #2: University, #3: Date, #4: Location, #5: Bullet Points
\newcommand{\cvEducation}[5]{
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#3}}
        \textbf{#1}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#4}}
        \textit{#2}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \vspace{0.10 cm}
    \begin{onecolentry}
        \begin{highlights} #5 \end{highlights}
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Category, #2: Skills List
\newcommand{\cvSkill}[2]{
    \begin{onecolentry}
        \textbf{#1:} #2
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.05 cm}
}

% #1: Title, #2: Tech Stack, #3: Date, #4: Subtitle/Type, #5: Bullet Points
\newcommand{\cvProject}[5]{
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#3}}
        \textbf{#1}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#4}}
        \textit{#2}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \vspace{0.10 cm}
    \begin{onecolentry}
        \begin{highlights} #5 \end{highlights}
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Title, #2: Organization, #3: Date, #4: Location, #5: Bullet Points
\newcommand{\cvExperience}[5]{
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#3}}
        \textbf{#1}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#4}}
        \textit{#2}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \vspace{0.10 cm}
    \begin{onecolentry}
        \begin{highlights} #5 \end{highlights}
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Title, #2: Authors, #3: Date, #4: Venue, #5: URL Text, #6: URL
\newcommand{\cvPublication}[6]{
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#3}}
        \parbox[t]{0.75\textwidth}{\textbf{#1}}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \vspace{0.10 cm}
    \begin{onecolentry}
        #2 \\
        \textit{#4} \ifthenelse{\equal{#6}{}}{}{ | \href{#6}{#5}}
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Cert Name, #2: Issuer, #3: Date, #4: Link Text, #5: Link URL
\newcommand{\cvCert}[5]{
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{Issued #3}}
        \textbf{#1}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{\href{#5}{#4}}}
        \textit{#2}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Position, #2: Date, #3: Event/Group, #4: Organization, #5: Bullet Points
\newcommand{\cvExtracurricularActivity}[5]{
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#2}}
        \textbf{#1}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#4}}
        \textit{#3}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \vspace{0.10 cm}
    \begin{onecolentry}
        \begin{highlights} #5 \end{highlights}
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Name, #2: Position, #3: Department, #4: Organization, #5: Email, #6: Phone
\newcommand{\cvReference}[6]{
    \begin{onecolentry}
        \textbf{#1} \\
        \textit{#2} \\
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#3}{}}{}{#3 \\}#4 \\
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#5}{}}{}{\href{mailto:#5}{#5}\ifthenelse{\equal{#6}{}}{}{\\}}%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#6}{}}{}{#6}
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

\section{Summary}
\cvSummary{Computer Science graduate and MSc student in ICT at BUET with strong foundations in Machine Learning, algorithms, and data analysis. Experienced in Python, scikit-learn, and Explainable AI (SHAP) through an undergraduate thesis and peer-reviewed publication on ML-based Intrusion Detection (COMPAS 2026). Certified in AI engineering and data science; eager to apply machine learning methodologies and software engineering skills to real-world AI projects.}

% PROJECTS
% ==============================================================================
\section{Projects}
\cvProject
    {Network Intrusion Detection System (NIDS) — Thesis}
    {Python, Scikit-Learn, Explainable AI (SHAP), Pandas}
    {2026}
    {COMPAS 2026, University of Dhaka}
    {
        \item Built a machine learning-based NIDS to classify network traffic and detect anomalous activity on the CICIIoT2025 dataset.
        \item Implemented a two-stage hierarchical model: fast triage first, followed by deeper diagnosis for flagged traffic, profiling computational overhead for edge deployment.
        \item Applied Explainable AI (XAI) using SHAP and LIME to interpret feature attributions and generate human-readable explanations of model decisions.
        \item Evaluated using strict chronological cross-validation, identifying and mitigating up to 21 percentage points of performance inflation from random-split data leakage.
    }

\cvProject
    {Augmented Reality (AR) Game with Hand Tracking}
    {Python, OpenCV}
    {Dec 2022}
    {Digital Innovation Fair, Patuakhali}
    {
        \item Developed an AR game using the OpenCV library in Python with real-time hand tracking and gesture-based interaction.
        \item Secured 2nd Place in the Open Category representing Patuakhali Science and Technology University.
    }

\cvProject
    {IoT-Based Smart Health Monitoring System}
    {Python, IoT, Real-Time Dashboard}
    {2024}
    {PSTU}
    {
        \item Built an IoT-based smart health monitoring system with real-time sensor data collection and an interactive dashboard for telemetry visualization.
    }

content_types = """<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8" standalone="yes"?>
<Types xmlns="http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/package/2006/content-types">
  <Default Extension="rels" ContentType="application/vnd.openxmlformats-package.relationships+xml"/>
  <Default Extension="xml" ContentType="application/xml"/>
  <Override PartName="/word/document.xml" ContentType="application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.wordprocessingml.document.main+xml"/>
  <Override PartName="/word/styles.xml" ContentType="application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.wordprocessingml.styles+xml"/>
</Types>
"""

rels = """<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8" standalone="yes"?>
<Relationships xmlns="http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/package/2006/relationships">
  <Relationship Id="rId1" Type="http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/officeDocument/2006/relationships/officeDocument" Target="word/document.xml"/>
</Relationships>
"""

document_rels = """<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8" standalone="yes"?>
<Relationships xmlns="http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/package/2006/relationships">
  <Relationship Id="rId1" Type="http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/officeDocument/2006/relationships/styles" Target="styles.xml"/>
</Relationships>
"""

styles_xml = """<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8" standalone="yes"?>
<w:styles xmlns:w="http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/wordprocessingml/2006/main">
  <w:docDefaults>
    <w:rPrDefault>
      <w:rPr>
        <w:rFonts w:ascii="Calibri" w:hAnsi="Calibri" w:cs="Calibri"/>
        <w:sz w:val="22"/>
        <w:lang w:val="en-US"/>
      </w:rPr>
    </w:rPrDefault>
  </w:docDefaults>
  <w:style w:type="paragraph" w:default="1" w:styleId="Normal">
    <w:name w:val="Normal"/>
    <w:qFormat/>
  </w:style>
</w:styles>
"""

output_path = r"c:\Users\avro18\Downloads\cv-resume-tex\AI_Internship_Application_Jyotirmoy_Avro.docx"

with zipfile.ZipFile(output_path, "w", zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED) as docx:
    docx.writestr("[Content_Types].xml", content_types)
    docx.writestr("_rels/.rels", rels)
    docx.writestr("word/_rels/document.xml.rels", document_rels)
    docx.writestr("word/styles.xml", styles_xml)
    docx.writestr("word/document.xml", doc_xml)

print(f"Generated successfully: {output_path}")

===== job_description.txt =====
Greetings from BdREN.

As part of our ongoing commitment to advancing education and research in Bangladesh, BdREN is pleased to offer internship opportunities (minimum duration of three months) to students of your esteemed department. We believe that bridging the gap between academic learning and practical application is essential to preparing students for successful professional careers. Through this initiative, students will have the opportunity to learn directly from our experienced engineers and specialists, gain hands-on experience with real-world projects, and develop the technical knowledge and practical skills needed to navigate the evolving ICT landscape.

The internship opportunities will cover Routing and Switching Infrastructure, Network Security, Software Development, Artificial Intelligence (AI), Cloud Management, and other relevant fields. Interested students may apply online with the URL https://survey.mindspear.app/949a052bde42 or by scanning the QR code (bottom right).

It should be noted that the honorarium will be allocated to the interns (based on their skills and performance) who successfully complete their tenure. The offer will remain valid as long as vacant intern positions are not filled.

We would appreciate your kind assistance in sharing this opportunity with your students and encouraging interested candidates to apply. We look forward to working with your department to support students in strengthening their technical capabilities and preparing for their future careers.

Thank you for your cooperation and support.

Sincerely,

Mohammad Tawrit

Chief Executive Officer (CEO)

Email: ceo@bdren.net.bd

Phone: 09666110022

    {
      "date_posted": "1mo",
      "post_text": "On October 9, we presented our paper at COMPAS 2026, hosted by the Department of Computer Science and Engineering, University of Dhaka.\n\nOur paper is titled “A Low-Complexity Hierarchical NIDS for IIoT: Profiling Computational Overhead and Mitigating Data Leakage.”\n\nIndustrial IoT needs intrusion detection that runs at the edge, not in the cloud. But much of the published work reports inflated accuracy because random cross-validation leaks future information into training.\n\nOur approach:\n  • A two-stage hierarchical NIDS: fast triage first, deeper diagnosis only for flagged traffic\n  • Strict chronological evaluation instead of random splits\n  • Profiled computational overhead to set an upper-bound baseline for edge feasibility\n\nOur key finding: random splitting inflated performance by up to 21 percentage points on DataSense (CICIIoT2025).\n\nThis is a single-dataset case study, so cross-dataset replication and embedded hardware validation come next.\n\nA big thank you to my co-author Mir Suhail Asarat for their hard work on this, and to Md Samsuzzaman sir for the guidance. Thanks also to the COMPAS 2026 organizers and everyone who stopped by with questions and feedback.\n\n#CyberSecurity #MachineLearning #IntrusionDetection #DataSense #CICIIoT2025 #NIDS #EdgeComputing #IIoT #COMPAS2026",
      "post_url": "https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7515333570807209984/"
    },
    {
      "date_posted": "2mo",
      "post_text": "Our paper \"A Low-Complexity Hierarchical NIDS for IIoT: Evaluating Edge Feasibility and Mitigating Temporal Data Leakage\" got accepted at IEEE COMPAS 2026, running October 9-10.\n\nCo-authored with Mir Suhail Asarat.\n\nMost IIoT intrusion detection papers report accuracy above 99%. We found out why: random cross-validation lets models train on future packets to predict past attacks. When we switched to a strict chronological split on the DataSense benchmark, the top models' Macro-F1 dropped sharply, purely from that leakage.\n\nSo we built a two-stage pipeline instead. Stage 1 is an XGBoost classifier plus network-layer heuristics that triages MQTT telemetry. Stage 2 only runs on flagged traffic: a DCNv2 model, small enough for a gateway with 1GB of RAM.\n\nWe also tested zero-day generalization by holding out each attack type during training. Recall stayed above 98% on Brute Force and MITM, but dropped to 26.70% on Malware, because MQTT telemetry alone can't see attacks that spread purely over the network layer. We reported that number instead of hiding it, since it marks exactly where this kind of detection stops working.\n\nThanks to Mir Suhail Asarat for the collaboration on this one.\n\n#IIoT #NetworkSecurity #IntrusionDetection #EdgeComputing #MachineLearning",

      "date_posted": "4mo • Edited",
      "post_text": "From a web server to full Active Directory Domain Admin...\n\nIt took me almost 4 hours to complete the Certified Red Team Analyst (CRTA) lab by Cyber Warfare Labs, and the multi-stage attack chain was an incredible learning experience. It simulated a realistic enterprise infrastructure spanning three network segments, requiring a complete shift from web application exploitation to advanced Active Directory forest abuse.\n\nHere is a quick breakdown of the kill chain:\n • External foothold: Exploit command injection in a PHP newsletter form, read the /etc/passwd GECOS field, and recover a plaintext SSH password.  \n • Pivoting: Extract domain credentials from the Firefox database history and open a Ligolo-ng tunnel into the internal network segment.  \n • Lateral movement: Log into the management workstation to dump LSA Secrets and extract a plaintext service account password. \n • Child domain compromise: Use a WMI shell on the child domain controller to run an offline ntdsutil IFM backup and retrieve the krbtgt AES-256 key.  \n • Forest dominance: Forge a cross-domain Kerberos ticket with injected Extra-SIDs to run a DCSync attack, extract the parent Administrator NTLM hash, and establish a PsExec SYSTEM shell.\n\nKey Red Teaming Takeaways:\n • Browser History is a Goldmine.\n • Modern tunneling tools like Ligolo-ng make navigating deep, multi-segmented environments incredibly efficient.\n • Active Directory child domains must be treated as security boundaries; if a child domain falls, the parent domain is often only one forged Kerberos ticket away.\n\n👇 Want to read the full, deep-dive technical breakdown, including the exact commands used, Nmap configurations, and the complete network diagram?\n\nCheck out my full writeup on Medium:\nhttps://lnkd.in/gE8yEQvC\n\n#RedTeaming #Cybersecurity #ActiveDirectory #PenetrationTesting #CRTA #Infosec #EthicalHacking",

    {
      "date_posted": "5mo",
      "post_text": "Imagine you apply for a job. The result comes back: “Rejected.” No explanation at all! Most ML models still work like this.\n\nXAI helps you understand the “gossip” of a model. And depending on what kind of gossip you want to hear, you choose either SHAP or LIME.\n\nFor the job selection example, SHAP explains it by saying your experience is low, your skills don’t match, and there are some gaps in your profile that led to the rejection.\n\nLIME works a bit differently here.\n\nLet’s say the same job application is explained using LIME. LIME first takes your profile and creates many “fake but similar” profiles around it. Then it checks if we slightly increase experience or fix a skill mismatch, does the result change from “Rejected” to “Selected”? By testing these small changes, LIME figures out which factors are actually influencing the decision the most.\n\nBasically, SHAP finds the culprits, while LIME shows what changes could potentially shift the outcome.\n\nWhy XAI is needed:\n • In cybersecurity, it helps explain why an activity is flagged as suspicious.\n • In healthcare, it helps justify risk predictions and detect bias or errors.\n • In finance, it helps explain loan or approval decisions and check whether the system is fair.",

      "post_text": "Sharing my experience of BrainStation23’s third-phase, day-long assessment for the Star Coder 2025 program.\n\nYesterday was the third phase of the Star Coder 2025 program by Brain Station 23. It was a day-long assessment held at their office in Mohakhali, Dhaka.\n\nA total of 124 applicants (112 Software Engineer applicants and 12 SQA Engineer applicants) participated in this phase.\n\nThe reporting time was 8:30 am at the office. After a brief welcome session, we were given an overview of the assessments.\n\nThe first test consisted of two personality tests: one on Truity and the other on BrainStation23’s exam portal.\n\nFollowing the first test, applicants were divided into groups of 6 through some fun games, with each group comprising 5 Software Engineer applicants and 1 SQA Engineer applicant. We were then given 6 tags—OwnPATH (team lead), Ownership, Passionate, Agility, Team Spirit, and Honesty—and were instructed to discuss within our team and assign the roles among ourselves over breakfast. I was honored to take on the OwnPATH (team lead) role in my team.\n\nFor the second test, each team was given 2 hours to design a system and solve some tasks on paper, which were then presented. The use of phones or the internet was not allowed. Two assessors (one from HR and another from the development team) assisted each team with clues and hints to solve the tasks and monitored team members on leadership, communication, teamwork, and passion.\n\nOur team’s task was to design an E-Ticketing system. We were given 5 tasks to complete:\n1. Requirement analysis\n2. System architecture flow\n3. Flowcharts for registration and login with specific constraints\n4. Pseudocode for some tasks involving roles and permissions\n5. Queries to retrieve specific data from the database we were to design.\n\nAfter two hours, we had lunch and presented our solution to the assessors.\n\nFor the third test, applicants were evaluated on their selected stacks. We were given 20 minutes to answer questions related to our chosen language/stack.\n\nFor the fourth and final test, applicants had one-on-one interviews where questions were asked about the skills and projects mentioned in their resumes during the application process.\n\nIt was a fun experience! We played games, enjoyed coffee, and met many talented individuals. Overall, it was a memorable day for me.",

    \begin{itemize}[
        topsep=0.10 cm,
        parsep=0.10 cm,
        partopsep=0pt,

    ]

\newenvironment{highlightsforbulletentries}{
    \begin{itemize}[
        topsep=0.10 cm,
        parsep=0.10 cm,
        partopsep=0pt,
        itemsep=0pt,
        leftmargin=10pt
    ]
}{

\newenvironment{onecolentry}{
    \begin{adjustwidth}{
        0 cm + 0.00001 cm
    }{
        0 cm + 0.00001 cm
    }
}{
    \end{adjustwidth}
} % new environment for one column entries

\newenvironment{twocolentry}[2][]{
    \onecolentry
    \def\secondColumn{#2}
    \setcolumnwidth{\fill, 4.5 cm}
    \begin{paracol}{2}
}{
    \switchcolumn \raggedleft \secondColumn
    \end{paracol}
    \endonecolentry
} % new environment for two column entries

\newenvironment{threecolentry}[3][]{
    \onecolentry
    \def\thirdColumn{#3}
    \setcolumnwidth{, \fill, 4.5 cm}
    \begin{paracol}{3}
    {\raggedright #2} \switchcolumn
}{
    \switchcolumn \raggedleft \thirdColumn
    \end{paracol}
    \endonecolentry
} % new environment for three column entries

\newenvironment{header}{
    \setlength{\topsep}{0pt}\par\kern\topsep\centering\linespread{1.5}
}{
    \par\kern\topsep
} % new environment for the header

===== README.md =====
# LaTeX CV-Resume Template

Clean, highly customizable, and ATS-friendly LaTeX Curriculum Vitae (CV) and Resume templates with support for a profile picture and semantic data commands.

## How to Create Your Own CV/Resume

We provide three different templates to choose from:
- **Resume Template** (`resume-template.tex` & `style-resume-template.sty`)
- **CV Template** (`cv-template.tex` & `style-cv-template.sty`)
- **CV Template with Picture** (`cv-picture-template.tex`, `style-cv-picture-template.sty` & `image.jpg`)

To build your own document, choose one of the templates and edit its corresponding `.tex` file. 

1. Copy or download your chosen `.tex` template and its matching `.sty` style file.
2. Open the `.tex` file in your preferred LaTeX editor.
3. Update the `\resumeHeader` (or `\cvHeader`), and subsequent sections with your personal information, work experience, education, etc.
4. Compile the document to generate your PDF.

Below are three popular ways to edit and compile your document.

### Option 1: Using Overleaf (Free Tier)

Overleaf is a great web-based LaTeX editor. You can use it for free without linking a GitHub account by uploading your files manually.

1. Create an account or log in at Overleaf.
2. Click on **New Project** and select **Blank Project**. Give it a name.
3. In the left-hand file tree, delete the default `main.tex` file.
4. Click the **Upload** icon (top left of the file tree) and upload the following files from your computer:
   - Your chosen `.tex` template file (e.g., `resume-template.tex`)
   - The matching `.sty` style file (e.g., `style-resume-template.sty`)
   - Include `image.jpg` if using the picture template
5. Change the main document setting so Overleaf knows which file to compile. Click the **Menu** button (top left), look for the **Main document** dropdown, and select your `.tex` file. *(Alternatively, you can just rename it to `main.tex`)*.
6. Click **Recompile** to generate and view your PDF!

### Option 2: Using Dev Containers (VS Code)

If you prefer an isolated local environment using Docker to avoid installing LaTeX directly onto your host OS, you can use Dev Containers.

1. Ensure you have Docker installed and running.
2. Install Visual Studio Code and the **Dev Containers** extension (`ms-vscode-remote.remote-containers`).
3. Open this folder in VS Code.
4. If prompted, click **Reopen in Container** (or press `F1` and run `Dev Containers: Reopen in Container`). This will build a container with LaTeX pre-installed (assuming a `.devcontainer` configuration is present in your project).
5. Once the container is built and running, open your `.tex` template file and compile using the integrated LaTeX tools provided by the container.

### Option 3: Local Setup with TeX Live and VS Code

For a fully local, native setup, you can install a LaTeX distribution directly on your machine.

1. Install **TeX Live** (or MacTeX for macOS, MiKTeX for Windows).
2. Install Visual Studio Code.
3. Install the **LaTeX Workshop** extension by James Yu (`james-yu.latex-workshop`) from the VS Code extension marketplace.
4. Open the folder containing your Resume files in VS Code.
5. Open your chosen `.tex` template file.
6. Press `Ctrl+Alt+B` (or `Cmd+Option+B` on Mac), or click the green "Play" (Build) icon in the top right corner of the editor to compile the document. The extension will automatically use your local TeX Live installation to generate the PDF.

===== resume-template.tex =====
\documentclass[11pt, a4paper]{article}
\usepackage{style-resume-template}

\begin{document}

% ==============================================================================
% HEADER
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Name, #2: Location URL, #3: Location Name, #4: Email, #5: Phone, #6: LinkedIn URL, #7: LinkedIn Name, #8: GitHub URL, #9: GitHub Name

\resumeHeader
    {Your Name}
    {https://map.location}
    {City}
    {email@example.com}
    {+1 234 567 890}
    {https://linkedin.com/in/yourprofile}
    {linkedin.com/in/yourprofile}
    {https://github.com/yourgithub}
    {github.com/yourgithub}

% --- Commented Example: Adding additional header links (e.g., Portfolio) ---
% Because \cvHeader already takes the maximum of 9 arguments, you can add 
% extra links like a Portfolio by uncommenting and modifying the example 
% block at the end of the \cvHeader definition in cv.sty.

% ==============================================================================
% SUMMARY / OBJECTIVE (Optional)
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Summary Text

\section{Summary / Objective}
\resumeSummary{Provide a brief 2-3 sentence summary or objective statement highlighting your core skills, experience level, and what you are looking for in your career.}

% ==============================================================================
% EDUCATION
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Degree, #2: University, #3: Date, #4: Location, #5: Bullet Points

\section{Education}
\resumeEducation
    {Degree Name in Major}
    {University or Institution Name}
    {Month, Year – Month, Year}
    {City, Country}
    {
        \item \textbf{Relevant Coursework:} Course 1, Course 2, Course 3, Course 4
        \item \textbf{Co-curriculars / Honors:} Club Name, Position, Honor Name
        \item \textbf{Thesis:} Thesis Title - Your contribution or speciality of in this thesis. (This is another way to add your Thesis)
    }

% ==============================================================================
% PUBLICATIONS / THESIS (Optional)
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Title, #2: Authors, #3: Date, #4: Venue, #5: URL Text, #6: URL

\section{Publications / Thesis}
\resumePublication
    {Title of the Publication or Thesis}
    {\textbf{Your Name}, Co-author 1, Co-author 2}
    {Month, Year}
    {Name of the Journal, Conference, or Institution}
    {DOI/Link}
    {https://doi.org/10.xxxx/xxxxx}

% ==============================================================================
% TECHNICAL SKILLS
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Category, #2: Skills List

\section{Technical Skills}
\resumeSkill{Category 1}{Skill 1, Skill 2, Skill 3, Skill 4, Skill 5}
\resumeSkill{Category 2}{Skill 1, Skill 2, Skill 3, Skill 4, Skill 5}
\resumeSkill{Category 3}{Skill 1, Skill 2, Skill 3, Skill 4, Skill 5}

% ==============================================================================
% PROJECTS
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Title, #2: Date, #3: Technology, #4: Subtitle/Type, #5: Bullet Points

\section{Projects}
\resumeProject
    {Project Title}
    {Month, Year – Month, Year}
    {Technology 1, Technology 2}
    {Project Type}
    {
        \item Action verb + what you did + how you did it + quantifiable result or impact.
        \item Highlight a specific technical implementation detail or problem solved.
    }

% ==============================================================================
% WORK EXPERIENCE (Optional)
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Title, #2: Date, #3: Company, #4: Location, #5: Bullet Points

\section{Work Experience}
\resumeExperience
    {Role Title}
    {Month, Year – Month, Year}
    {Company / Organization}
    {City, Country}
    {
        \item Action verb + what you did + how you did it + quantifiable result or impact.
        \item Highlight a specific technical implementation detail or problem solved.
        \item Another bullet point highlighting a key contribution or feature.
    }

% ==============================================================================
% EXTRACURRICULAR ACTIVITIES (Optional)
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Position, #2: Date, #3: Event/Group, #4: Organization, #5: Bullet Points

\section{Extracurricular Activities}
\resumeExtracurricularActivity
    {Role or Position Title}
    {Month, Year – Month, Year}
    {Event or Sub-group}
    {Club / Organization}
    {
        \item Describe your involvement, responsibilities, and achievements.
        \item Highlight specific events organized or participated in.
    }

% ==============================================================================
% CERTIFICATIONS & AWARDS (Optional)
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Cert Name, #2: Issuer, #3: Date, #4: Link Text, #5: Link URL

\section{Certifications}
\resumeCert
    {Award or Certification Name}
    {Certificate Issuer}
    {Month, Year}
    {Verify Credential}
    {https://link-to-credential.com}

\end{document}

===== resume.tex.previous =====
\documentclass[11pt, a4paper]{article}
\usepackage{style-resume-template}

\begin{document}

% ==============================================================================
% HEADER
% ==============================================================================

\makeheader{Your Name}{
    \contactItem{https://map.location}{\atsIcon{Location: }{\faMapMarker*}}{City}{}
    \contactSeparator
    \contactItem{mailto:email@example.com}{\atsIcon{Email: }{\faEnvelope[regular]}}{email@example.com}{}
    \contactSeparator
    \contactItem{tel:+1234567890}{\atsIcon{Phone: }{\faPhone*}}{+1 234 567 890}{}
    \contactSeparator
    \contactItem{https://linkedin.com/in/yourprofile}{\atsIcon{LinkedIn: }{\faLinkedinIn}}{linkednin.com/in/yourprofile}{}
    \contactSeparator
    \contactItem{https://github.com/yourgithub}{\atsIcon{GitHub: }{\faGithub}}{github.com/yourgithub}{}
}

% ==============================================================================
% SUMMARY / OBJECTIVE (Optional)
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Summary Text

\section{Summary / Objective}
\resumeSummary{Provide a brief 2-3 sentence summary or objective statement highlighting your core skills, experience level, and what you are looking for in your career.}

% ==============================================================================
% EDUCATION
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Degree, #2: University, #3: Date, #4: Location, #5: Bullet Points

\section{Education}
\resumeEducation
    {Degree Name in Major}
    {University or Institution Name}
    {Month, Year – Month, Year}
    {City, Country}
    {
        \item \textbf{Relevant Coursework:} Course 1, Course 2, Course 3, Course 4
        \item \textbf{Co-curriculars / Honors:} Club Name, Position, Honor Name
        \item \textbf{Thesis:} Thesis Title - Your contribution or speciality of in this thesis. (This is another way to add your Thesis)
    }

% ==============================================================================
% PUBLICATIONS / THESIS (Optional)
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Publication Title, #2: Authors, #3: Date, #4: Venue, #5: URL Text, #6: URL

\resumePublication
    {Title of the Publication or Thesis}
    {\textbf{Your Name}, Co-author 1, Co-author 2}

    {https://doi.org/10.xxxx/xxxxx}

% ==============================================================================
% #1: Category, #2: Skills List

\section{Technical Skills}
\resumeSkill{Category 1}{Skill 1, Skill 2, Skill 3, Skill 4, Skill 5}
\resumeSkill{Category 2}{Skill 1, Skill 2, Skill 3, Skill 4, Skill 5}
\resumeSkill{Category 3}{Skill 1, Skill 2, Skill 3, Skill 4, Skill 5}

% ==============================================================================

% ==============================================================================
% #1: Title, #2: Date, #3: Technology, #4: Subtitle/Type, #5: Bullet Points

\section{Projects}
\resumeProject
    {Project Title}
    {Month, Year – Month, Year}
    {Technology 1, Technology 2}
    {Project Type}
    {
        \item Action verb + what you did + how you did it + quantifiable result or impact.
        \item Highlight a specific technical implementation detail or problem solved.
    }

% ==============================================================================
% WORK EXPERIENCE (Optional)
% ==============================================================================
% #1: Title, #2: Date, #3: Company, #4: Location, #5: Bullet Points

\section{Work Experience}
\resumeExperience
    {Role Title}
    {Month, Year – Month, Year}
    {Company / Organization}
    {City, Country}
    {
        \item Action verb + what you did + how you did it + quantifiable result or impact.
        \item Highlight a specific technical implementation detail or problem solved.
        \item Another bullet point highlighting a key contribution or feature.
    }

% ==============================================================================

% ==============================================================================
% #1: Position, #2: Date, #3: Event/Group, #4: Organization, #5: Bullet Points

\section{Extracurricular Activities}

    {Role or Position Title}
    {Month, Year – Month, Year}
    {Event or Sub-group}
    {Club / Organization}
    {
        \item Describe your involvement, responsibilities, and achievements.
        \item Highlight specific events organized or participated in.
    }

% ==============================================================================

% ==============================================================================

===== style-cv-picture-template.sty =====
\NeedsTeXFormat{LaTeX2e}
\ProvidesPackage{style-cv-picture-template}[2026/06/08 Abstracted CV Style]

% --- Core Packages ---
\RequirePackage[
    ignoreheadfoot, % Set margins without considering header and footer
    top=1in, bottom=1in, left=0.75in, right=0.75in
    % top=1in, bottom=1in, left=1in, right=1in
]{geometry}
\RequirePackage{titlesec} % For customizing section titles
\RequirePackage{tabularx} % For tables
\RequirePackage{array}
\RequirePackage[dvipsnames]{xcolor} % For text colors
\RequirePackage{enumitem} % For list customization
\RequirePackage{fontawesome5} % For icons
\RequirePackage{accsupp} % For ATS text replacement
\RequirePackage{amsmath}
\RequirePackage{calc}
\RequirePackage{changepage} % For single column entries
\RequirePackage{paracol} % For two column layouts
\RequirePackage{ifthen}
\RequirePackage{needspace} % Prevent page breaks after titles
\RequirePackage{iftex}
\RequirePackage{graphicx}

% --- ATS Parsable PDF Setup ---
\ifPDFTeX
    \input{glyphtounicode}
    \pdfgentounicode=1
    \RequirePackage[utf8]{inputenc}
    % \RequirePackage{lmodern}
    \RequirePackage{newtxtext,newtxmath} % Loads Times New Roman for text & math
\fi

% --- Colors & Links ---
\definecolor{primaryColor}{RGB}{0, 79, 144}
\RequirePackage[
    colorlinks=true,
    urlcolor=primaryColor
]{hyperref}

% Custom link command with an external arrow icon
\let\hrefWithoutArrow\href
% \renewcommand{\href}[2]{\hrefWithoutArrow{#1}{\ifthenelse{\equal{#2}{}}{ }{#2 }\raisebox{.15ex}{\footnotesize \faExternalLink*}}}

% --- Global Formatting ---
\pagestyle{empty} % Remove page numbers
\setcounter{secnumdepth}{0} % No section numbers
\setlength{\parindent}{0pt} % Remove indentation
\setlength{\topskip}{0pt}
\setlength{\columnsep}{0cm} % Column separation for paracol
\AtBeginEnvironment{adjustwidth}{\partopsep0pt}

% --- Section Styling ---
\titleformat{\section}{\needspace{4\baselineskip}\bfseries\large}{}{0pt}{}[\vspace{1pt}\titlerule]
\titlespacing{\section}{0pt}{0.15cm}{0.2cm}

% --- Content Environments (Hidden from main file) ---
\newenvironment{cvheader}{
    \setlength{\topsep}{0pt}\par\kern\topsep\raggedright\linespread{1.5}
}{\par\kern\topsep}

\renewcommand\labelitemi{$\circ$} % Custom bullet points

\newenvironment{highlights}{
    \begin{itemize}[topsep=0.05cm, parsep=0.03cm, partopsep=0pt, itemsep=0pt, leftmargin=0.4cm+10pt]
}{\end{itemize}}

\newenvironment{onecolentry}{
    \begin{adjustwidth}{0.2cm+0.00001cm}{0.2cm+0.00001cm}
}{\end{adjustwidth}}

\newenvironment{twocolentry}[2][]{
    \onecolentry
    \def\secondColumn{#2}
    \setcolumnwidth{\fill, 6cm}
    \begin{paracol}{2}
}{
    \switchcolumn \raggedleft \secondColumn
    \end{paracol}
    \endonecolentry
}

% --- ATS-Friendly Icon Wrapper ---
\newcommand{\atsIcon}[2]{%
    \BeginAccSupp{method=pdfstringdef,unicode,ActualText=#1}%
    #2%
    \EndAccSupp{}%
}

% ==============================================================================
% SEMANTIC DATA COMMANDS (To be used in cv.tex)
% ==============================================================================

% Profile Picture Variable Setup
\newcommand{\cvpic}{}
\newcommand{\cvProfilePic}[1]{\renewcommand{\cvpic}{#1}}

% Header Variables
\newcommand{\contactItem}[3]{%
    \mbox{\hrefWithoutArrow{#1}{\color{black}{\footnotesize #2}\hspace*{0.10cm}#3}}%
}

% Header Variables - with hidden ATS labels
% \newcommand{\contactItem}[4]{%
%     \mbox{\hrefWithoutArrow{#1}{\color{black}{\footnotesize #2}\hspace*{0.10cm}#3\phantom{\footnotesize#4}}}%
% }

\newsavebox\ANDbox
\sbox\ANDbox{}
\newcommand{\contactSeparator}{%
    \kern 0.15cm\unskip\cleaders\copy\ANDbox\hskip\wd\ANDbox\ignorespaces\kern 0.15cm%
}

\newcommand{\makeheader}[2]{
    \begin{cvheader}
        \begin{minipage}[c]{\textwidth-3.5cm}
            \raggedright
            \textbf{\fontsize{24 pt}{24 pt}\selectfont #1}\\[0.3cm]
            \fontsize{9.5}{11}\selectfont #2
        \end{minipage}%
        \hfill
        \begin{minipage}[c]{3cm}
            \raggedleft
            \ifthenelse{\equal{\cvpic}{}}{}{%
                {\setlength{\fboxsep}{0pt}\setlength{\fboxrule}{0.15pt}\color{black!35}\fbox{\includegraphics[width=\dimexpr 3cm - 0.3pt\relax]{\cvpic}}}%
            }
        \end{minipage}
    \end{cvheader}
    \vspace{0.2cm}
}

\newboolean{isFirstContactItem}
% #1: Name, #2: Location URL, #3: Location Name, #4: Email, #5: Phone, #6: LinkedIn URL, #7: LinkedIn Name, #8: GitHub URL, #9: GitHub Name
\newcommand{\cvHeader}[9]{
    \hypersetup{pdftitle={CV of #1}, pdfauthor={#1}}%
    \setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{true}%
    \makeheader{#1}{%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#3}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{#2}{\atsIcon{Location: }{\faMapMarker*}}{#3}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#4}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{mailto:#4}{\atsIcon{Email: }{\faEnvelope[regular]}}{#4}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#5}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{tel:#5}{\atsIcon{Phone: }{\faPhone*}}{#5}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#7}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{#6}{\atsIcon{LinkedIn: }{\faLinkedinIn}}{#7}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#9}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{#8}{\atsIcon{GitHub: }{\faGithub}}{#9}%
        }%
        % --- Commented example: Adding a custom entry like a portfolio link ---
        % % Uncomment and modify the lines below to add a portfolio or other link
        % \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
        % \contactItem{https://yourportfolio.com}{\atsIcon{Portfolio: }{\faGlobe}}{yourportfolio.com}%
        % ----------------------------------------------------------------------
    }%
}

% #1: Summary Text
\newcommand{\cvSummary}[1]{
    \begin{onecolentry}
        #1
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Degree, #2: University, #3: Date, #4: Location, #5: Bullet Points
\newcommand{\cvEducation}[5]{
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#3}}
        \textbf{#1}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#4}}
        \textit{#2}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \vspace{0.10 cm}
    \begin{onecolentry}
        \begin{highlights} #5 \end{highlights}
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Category, #2: Skills List
\newcommand{\cvSkill}[2]{
    \begin{onecolentry}
        \textbf{#1:} #2
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.05 cm}
}

% #1: Title, #2: Tech Stack, #3: Date, #4: Subtitle/Type, #5: Bullet Points
\newcommand{\cvProject}[5]{
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#3}}
        \textbf{#1}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#4}}
        \textit{#2}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \vspace{0.10 cm}
    \begin{onecolentry}
        \begin{highlights} #5 \end{highlights}
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Title, #2: Organization, #3: Date, #4: Location, #5: Bullet Points
\newcommand{\cvExperience}[5]{
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#3}}
        \textbf{#1}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#4}}
        \textit{#2}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \vspace{0.10 cm}
    \begin{onecolentry}
        \begin{highlights} #5 \end{highlights}
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Title, #2: Authors, #3: Date, #4: Venue, #5: URL Text, #6: URL
\newcommand{\cvPublication}[6]{
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#3}}
        \parbox[t]{0.75\textwidth}{\textbf{#1}}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \vspace{0.10 cm}
    \begin{onecolentry}
        #2 \\
        \textit{#4} \ifthenelse{\equal{#6}{}}{}{ | \href{#6}{#5}}
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Cert Name, #2: Issuer, #3: Date, #4: Link Text, #5: Link URL
\newcommand{\cvCert}[5]{
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{Issued #3}}
        \textbf{#1}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{\href{#5}{#4}}}
        \textit{#2}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Position, #2: Date, #3: Event/Group, #4: Organization, #5: Bullet Points
\newcommand{\cvExtracurricularActivity}[5]{
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#2}}
        \textbf{#1}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \begin{twocolentry}{\textit{#4}}
        \textit{#3}
    \end{twocolentry}
    \vspace{0.10 cm}
    \begin{onecolentry}
        \begin{highlights} #5 \end{highlights}
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

% #1: Name, #2: Position, #3: Department, #4: Organization, #5: Email, #6: Phone
\newcommand{\cvReference}[6]{
    \begin{onecolentry}
        \textbf{#1} \\
        \text{#2} \\
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#3}{}}{}{#3 \\}#4 \\
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#5}{}}{}{\href{mailto:#5}{#5}\ifthenelse{\equal{#6}{}}{}{\\}}%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#6}{}}{}{#6}
    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}
}

===== style-cv-template.sty =====
\NeedsTeXFormat{LaTeX2e}
\ProvidesPackage{style-cv-template}[2026/06/08 Abstracted CV Style]

% --- Core Packages ---
\RequirePackage[
    ignoreheadfoot, % Set margins without considering header and footer
    top=1in, bottom=1in, left=0.75in, right=0.75in
    % top=1in, bottom=1in, left=1in, right=1in
]{geometry}
\RequirePackage{titlesec} % For customizing section titles
\RequirePackage{tabularx} % For tables
\RequirePackage{array}
\RequirePackage[dvipsnames]{xcolor} % For text colors
\RequirePackage{enumitem} % For list customization
\RequirePackage{fontawesome5} % For icons
\RequirePackage{accsupp} % For ATS text replacement
\RequirePackage{amsmath}
\RequirePackage{calc}
\RequirePackage{changepage} % For single column entries
\RequirePackage{paracol} % For two column layouts
\RequirePackage{ifthen}
\RequirePackage{needspace} % Prevent page breaks after titles
\RequirePackage{iftex}

% --- ATS Parsable PDF Setup ---
\ifPDFTeX
    \input{glyphtounicode}
    \pdfgentounicode=1
    \RequirePackage[utf8]{inputenc}
    % \RequirePackage{lmodern}
    \RequirePackage{newtxtext,newtxmath} % Loads Times New Roman for text & math
\fi

% --- Colors & Links ---
\definecolor{primaryColor}{RGB}{0, 79, 144}
\RequirePackage[
    colorlinks=true,
    urlcolor=primaryColor
]{hyperref}

% Custom link command with an external arrow icon
\let\hrefWithoutArrow\href
% \renewcommand{\href}[2]{\hrefWithoutArrow{#1}{\ifthenelse{\equal{#2}{}}{ }{#2 }\raisebox{.15ex}{\footnotesize \faExternalLink*}}}

% --- Global Formatting ---
\pagestyle{empty} % Remove page numbers
\setcounter{secnumdepth}{0} % No section numbers
\setlength{\parindent}{0pt} % Remove indentation
\setlength{\topskip}{0pt}
\setlength{\columnsep}{0cm} % Column separation for paracol
\AtBeginEnvironment{adjustwidth}{\partopsep0pt}

% --- Section Styling ---
\titleformat{\section}{\needspace{4\baselineskip}\bfseries\large}{}{0pt}{}[\vspace{1pt}\titlerule]
\titlespacing{\section}{0pt}{0.15cm}{0.2cm}

% --- Content Environments (Hidden from main file) ---
\newenvironment{cvheader}{
    \setlength{\topsep}{0pt}\par\kern\topsep\centering\linespread{1.5}
}{\par\kern\topsep}

\renewcommand\labelitemi{$\circ$} % Custom bullet points

\newenvironment{highlights}{
    \begin{itemize}[topsep=0.05cm, parsep=0.03cm, partopsep=0pt, itemsep=0pt, leftmargin=0.4cm+10pt]
}{\end{itemize}}

\newenvironment{onecolentry}{
    \begin{adjustwidth}{0.2cm+0.00001cm}{0.2cm+0.00001cm}
}{\end{adjustwidth}}

\newenvironment{twocolentry}[2][]{
    \onecolentry
    \def\secondColumn{#2}
    \setcolumnwidth{\fill, 6cm}
    \begin{paracol}{2}
}{
    \switchcolumn \raggedleft \secondColumn
    \end{paracol}
    \endonecolentry
}

% --- ATS-Friendly Icon Wrapper ---
\newcommand{\atsIcon}[2]{%
    \BeginAccSupp{method=pdfstringdef,unicode,ActualText=#1}%
    #2%
    \EndAccSupp{}%
}

% ==============================================================================
% SEMANTIC DATA COMMANDS (To be used in cv.tex)
% ==============================================================================

% Header Variables
\newcommand{\contactItem}[3]{%
    \mbox{\hrefWithoutArrow{#1}{\color{black}{\footnotesize #2}\hspace*{0.10cm}#3}}%
}

% Header Variables - with hidden ATS labels
% \newcommand{\contactItem}[4]{%
%     \mbox{\hrefWithoutArrow{#1}{\color{black}{\footnotesize #2}\hspace*{0.10cm}#3\phantom{\footnotesize#4}}}%
% }

\newsavebox\ANDbox
\sbox\ANDbox{}
\newcommand{\contactSeparator}{%
    \kern 0.15cm\unskip\cleaders\copy\ANDbox\hskip\wd\ANDbox\ignorespaces\kern 0.15cm%
}

\newcommand{\makeheader}[2]{
    \begin{cvheader}
        \textbf{\fontsize{24 pt}{24 pt}\selectfont #1}
        \vspace{0.3 cm}
        
        \fontsize{9.5}{11}\selectfont #2
    \end{cvheader}
    \vspace{0.2cm}
}

\newboolean{isFirstContactItem}
% #1: Name, #2: Location URL, #3: Location Name, #4: Email, #5: Phone, #6: LinkedIn URL, #7: LinkedIn Name, #8: GitHub URL, #9: GitHub Name
\newcommand{\cvHeader}[9]{
    \hypersetup{pdftitle={CV of #1}, pdfauthor={#1}}%
    \setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{true}%
    \makeheader{#1}{%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#3}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{#2}{\atsIcon{Location: }{\faMapMarker*}}{#3}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#4}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{mailto:#4}{\atsIcon{Email: }{\faEnvelope[regular]}}{#4}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#5}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{tel:#5}{\atsIcon{Phone: }{\faPhone*}}{#5}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#7}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{#6}{\atsIcon{LinkedIn: }{\faLinkedinIn}}{#7}%
        }%
        \ifthenelse{\equal{#9}{}}{}{%
            \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
            \contactItem{#8}{\atsIcon{GitHub: }{\faGithub}}{#9}%
        }%
        % --- Commented example: Adding a custom entry like a portfolio link ---
        % % Uncomment and modify the lines below to add a portfolio or other link
        % \ifthenelse{\boolean{isFirstContactItem}}{\setboolean{isFirstContactItem}{false}}{\contactSeparator}%
        % \contactItem{https://yourportfolio.com}{\atsIcon{Portfolio: }{\faGlobe}}{yourportfolio.com}%
        % ----------------------------------------------------------------------
    }%
}

    \end{onecolentry}
    \vspace{0.2 cm}

}

]{geometry}

    \begin{adjustwidth}{0.2cm+0.00001cm}{0.2cm+0.00001cm}
}{\end{adjustwidth}}

    \end{paracol}

===== university_courses/Course_Topics.md =====
# Course Topics (Concise Summary)

## Level I
### Semester I
- **PHY 111 (Physics-I):** Details missing.
- **PHY 112 (Physics-I Sessional):** Details missing.
- **CHE 111 (Chemistry):** Atomic structure, chemical bonds, solutions & phase diagrams, thermodynamics, chemical kinetics.
- **CHE 112 (Chemistry Sessional):** Laboratory works based on CHE 112.
- **MAT 111 (Mathematics-I):** Differential calculus, matrix algebra, co-ordinate geometry.
- **EEE 111 (Basic Electrical Engineering):** DC/AC circuits, electromagnetism, network analysis.
- **EEE 112 (Basic Electrical Engineering Sessional):** Laboratory works.
- **CIT 111 (Computer Fundamentals and Programming with C/C++):** OS basics, C/C++ structured programming, arrays/pointers, memory allocation.
- **CIT 112 (Computer Fundamentals and Programming with C/C++ Sessional):** Sessional work.
- **CCE 114 (Engineering Drawing):** Isometric/sectional views, CAD projects.

### Semester II
- **PHY 121 (Physics-II):** Details missing.
- **PHY 122 (Physics-II Sessional):** Details missing.
- **MAT 121 (Mathematics-II):** Complex variables, Laplace transforms, difference equations.
- **CIT 121 (Discrete Mathematics):** Mathematical logic, predicate calculus, graph theory, trees, group theory.
- **LCM 121 (Communicative English):** Details missing.
- **EEE 121 (Electronic Device and Circuits):** Semiconductors, BJTs, FETs, OPAMPs, feedback/oscillators.
- **EEE 122 (Electronic Device and Circuits Sessional):** Laboratory works.
- **CCE 121 (Object Oriented Programming):** OOP concepts (Java), inheritance, polymorphism, GUI, multi-threading.
- **CCE 122 (Object Oriented Programming Sessional):** Laboratory works.
- **CCE 124 (Computer Programming Contest-I):** Programming contest.

## Level II
### Semester I
- **CIT 211 (Data Structure and Algorithms):** Stacks, queues, linked lists, trees, graphs, sorting, and algorithmic complexity.
- **CIT 212 (Data Structure and Algorithms Sessional):** Sessional work.
- **CIT 213 (Software Engineering):** Software process models, design techniques, program testing, project management.
- **CCE 211 (Data Communication and Engineering):** OSI protocol architecture, physical layer, wireless transmission, multiplexing, data link control.
- **MAT 211 (Mathematics-III):** Probability distributions, hypothesis testing, correlation/regression, statistics.
- **EEE 211 (Electrical Technology):** Three-phase circuits, AC/DC motors, instrumentation amplifiers, transducers.
- **EEE 212 (Electronic Devices and Circuits Sessional):** Laboratory works.
- **AIS 211:** Details missing.

### Semester II
- **CCE 221 (Digital Logic Design):** Boolean algebra, logic gates, combinational/sequential logic, flip-flops, counters.
- **CCE 222 (Digital Logic Design Sessional):** Laboratory works.
- **CCE 223 (Database System):** Relational data model, SQL, normalization (ER modeling), query optimization.
- **CCE 224 (Database System Sessional):** Laboratory works.
- **AES 221:** Details missing.
- **MAT 221 (Mathematics-IV):** Fourier series, Fourier transforms, vector analysis.
- **CIT 220 (Web Programming Project):** HTML/CSS, JavaScript, PHP, MySQL, frameworks (Joomla, Codeigniter).
- **CIT 221 (Information System Analysis and Design):** SDLC, system requirements determination, structured systems analysis & design.
- **CIT 222 (Information System Analysis and Design Sessional):** Sessional work.
- **CIT 224 (Computer Programming Contest-II):** Programming contest.

## Level III
### Semester I
- **CIT 311 (Microprocessors and Assembly Language):** Microprocessor architecture, assembly programming, I/O interfacing, 8051 microcontroller.
- **CIT 312 (Microprocessors and Assembly Language Sessional):** Sessional work.
- **CIT 313 (Computer Organization and Architecture):** Cache memory, pipelined processors, RISC/CISC, multiprocessors.
- **CIT 315 (Artificial Intelligence):** AI problem solving, knowledge representation (Logic/PROLOG), machine learning, neural networks.
- **CIT 316 (Artificial Intelligence Sessional):** Sessional work.
- **CCE 310 (Software Development Project-I):** Project based on C/C++ or C#.
- **CCE 311 (Numerical Methods):** Solutions of algebraic/transcendental equations, interpolation, numerical integration, differential equations.
- **CCE 312 (Numerical Methods Sessional):** Laboratory works.
- **CCE 313 (Computer Networks):** Physical, data link, MAC, network, transport, and application layers; routing & congestion control.
- **CCE 314 (Computer Networks Sessional):** Laboratory works.

### Semester II
- **CIT 320 (Software Development Project-II):** Project in J2SE, Java Swing, and Database.
- **CIT 321 (Operating System):** Process synchronization, CPU scheduling, memory management (paging), file systems, deadlocks.
- **CIT 322 (Operating System Sessional):** Sessional work.
- **CIT 323 (Simulation and Modeling):** Random number generation, statistical analysis, procedural/solid modeling.
- **CIT 324 (Simulation and Modeling Sessional):** Sessional work.
- **EEE 321 (Digital Electronics and Pulse Techniques):** Logic families (TTL/CMOS), AD/DA converters, wave shaping, pulse generators.
- **EEE 322 (Digital Electronics and Pulse Techniques Sessional):** Laboratory works.
- **CCE 320 (Computer Programming Contest-III):** Programming contest.
- **CCE 321 (Computer Peripheral and Interfacing):** Sensors/transducers, memory/IO interfacing, RS-232/IEEE-488 buses.
- **CCE 322 (Computer Peripheral and Interfacing Sessional):** Laboratory works.
- **CCE 323:** Details missing.

## Level IV
### Semester I
- **CSE 410:** Details missing.
- **CSE 412:** Details missing.
- **CCE 411 (Algorithm Engineering):** Computational complexity, approximation/randomized algorithms, combinatorial optimization.
- **CCE 413 (VLSI Design):** CMOS fabrication/gates, hardware modeling, architectural synthesis, FPGA.
- **CCE 415 (Network Routing and Switching):** LAN technologies, switch security, IP addressing, Cisco routing protocols (WANs).
- **CCE 416:** Details missing.
- **CCE 417 (Data Warehousing and Mining):** OLAP, data preprocessing, association rules (Apriori), classification & clustering.
- **CIT 411 (Compiler Design and Automata Theory):** Finite automata, lexical/syntax analysis, intermediate code, code optimization.
- **CIT 412 (Compiler Design and Automata Theory Sessional):** Sessional work.

### Semester II
- **CSE 420:** Details missing.
- **CSE 421:** Details missing.
- **CCE 421 (Cryptography and Network Security):** Symmetric/Public-key cryptography, digital signatures, Kerberos, email/web/IP security.
- **CCE 422:** Details missing.
- **CIT 421 (Digital Signal Processing):** Discrete time signals, Fourier/Z-transforms, digital filter design.
- **CIT 422 (Digital Signal Processing Sessional):** Sessional work.
- **CIT 423 (OPTIONAL : Robotics and Computer Vision):** Robot kinematics, trajectory planning, image processing, object recognition.
